# Campaign of 2026-09-30, campaign tier, present arm, discovery

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:1c21e55eb09aca89b3d736ac79f8accfc64ddd7853bace5fb5e577a82d794bb9
lock: sha256:4b95fd419a3824634b9644c91b13356ba9b71567f813445913c430e3aa12c13a
selection: sha256:8ec515cf6d769b34f8fc840d00f902606dc575c74a4105c7599670b563be6ad5
read_set: sha256:c563ff36d54e56139a6d1ca7cca03fbe175864970d6fcacabe5144cfcaaaffc9
seed: 0
harness: 0.5.0
tier: campaign
arm: present
at: 2026-09-30
cost_cents: 3686
```

**This is the full re-run of the counterfactual campaign that [#1384](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1384) asked for.** `tools/probe/campaign.sh` recorded it on 2026-09-30 (UTC), at the 30 repetitions per probe that the tier declares, after the changes to the recorder, the grader, the read set and the seal had merged (#1394, #1396, #1414 and #1413) and after #1448 had merged. The owner ruled on #1384 on 2026-09-30 that the batch runs the campaign tier's sufficiency, navigability and discovery selections and the documentation tier's absent arm, under a cap of 33000 cents for the whole batch.

**One batch, one tree.** Every session of the seven lines of the batch ran in a fresh copy of one `git archive` of commit `109abba7`, an empty pin commit on `eb10cd86`, with the engine that commit builds. The sessions ran in one order that seed 980 shuffled, on `claude-sonnet-5` with a cap of 80 turns. The first 186 sessions ran four at a time. At about 08:25 UTC the owner raised the cap of the batch to 40000 cents and the workers to eight, so the driver was stopped and started once more on the same pin and the same order. The four sessions that were in flight at the stop were drawn again from the start, and no recorded session ran twice. The cap stopped 1 of the 60 sessions of this line. Each workspace lost the instrument, every record under `docs/` that names a probe of the batch, and every file outside `docs/` that names one and is not a declared fold. Each absent workspace also lost the ablation of its tier. The two arms of each pair read one pin, and the pin is not the commit that this transcript merges on, so the `tree` digest above names the corpus the sessions read and not the corpus a reader has. The `seed` in the identity above is the plan's seed, and the shuffle seed is not a member of the identity.

**The oracle.** A `patched` artifact of either arm was checked in a copy of the present tree with the artifact written into it (`probe-transform.sh --oracle-tree`), so one oracle graded both arms. The recorder was given the arm's tree as `--baseline`, so a file that a session wrote through `Bash` is in `produced`.

**It succeeds no transcript.** The campaign of 2026-09-28 recorded no discovery line. The line excludes `HW-PROBE-the-authoring-skill-reaches-an-agent-that-is-about-to-write-a-governed-document`, because the plan refuses it: that probe expects a predicate over `.claude/skills/headwater-authoring/SKILL.md`, and the absent arm of this tier removes `.claude`.

**What the seal cannot find.** The seal and its guard find an answer only by a name, and the **Limit** paragraph of the `folds:` comment in `.headwater/probe.yml` states that bound. In the sealed present tree, 69 of the 529 entries of `.headwater/nav.yml` name a file that the tree does not hold. Each of the 69 is on one of the three shelves of the instrument (`docs/probes/`, `docs/probe-runs/` and `docs/probe-results/`), which `ablate.sh --present` removes and the seal does not edit out of that fold. So a present-arm session can read the title of each probe in `nav.yml` and cannot open the file. No absent arm holds `nav.yml`. This is a difference between the arms that nothing controls.

**What the workspace did not hold in.** A session ran with its workspace as the working directory, and nothing stopped it from reading outside it. 5 of the 60 sessions of this line made a call that names a path outside the workspace, in the checkout of this repository or in the directory that held the batch, and 0 of them named the checkout. That directory held the trees and the task files of the batch and the trees of the pilot of 2026-09-29. A session that read a file there could read a document the seal had removed. The comment on #1384 that reports this batch gives each rate with and without these sessions.

Find the general mechanism in the engine crates for how a \\\"check rule\\\" is registered — is there a trait like `Rule` or `Check`, a registry file listing all rules, an enum of rule IDs, etc.? Give the file path and a short code excerpt.\\n\\n4. Look for any existing rule that already checks for an \\\"empty\\\" field (search for \\\"empty\\\" or \\\"is_empty\\\" in engine/ and docs/acceptance-criteria/ and taxonomy-source/) to see the established pattern for \\\"field must not be empty\\\" rules, if one exists.\\n\\n5. Confirm whether severity classification (error vs advisory) is itself declared in the taxonomy/docs, or hardcoded in the engine, per the repo's CLAUDE.md rule: \\\"A rule is an error when its remedy is mechanical and total... A rule is advisory when the remedy is a rewrite.\\\"\\n\\nReport concisely with exact file paths and line numbers so I can go edit the right files directly. Don't make any edits — this is read-only investigation.\"}"
